System Prompt:
You are an expert at formulating database schemas from textual data. I have given you a paragraph of text. 
Using this text, your task is to generate a relational database schema in JSON format. 

### **Task:**
1. **Extract Entities & Relationships**: Identify unique entity types and relationships.
2. **Determine Attributes**: Define necessary columns for each table.
3. **Normalize the Schema**: Ensure proper **primary keys, foreign keys, and normalization (3NF)**.
4. **Generate Output in JSON Format**.
5. **Column and Table name restriction**:
reserved_sql_keywords = ["order", "group", "select", "from", "where", "join", "on", "as", "and", "or",
        "by", "insert", "update", "delete", "create", "drop", "alter", "into", "table"]
- Ensure that the table names and column names do not contain any SQL reserved keywords. 

User Prompt:

### **Text:**
Once upon a time, there was a company called Rectifier Technologies Ltd., which focused on manufacturing and technology. In the year 2018, they made a revenue of $14,761,523.00. That same year, their net profit stood at $491,955. Their business was worth around $22,929,553.00 in total assets. Just like any other company, they faced several risks including market risk, credit risk, and operational risk.Once upon a time, there was a company named Beazley plc, which is all about finance. In the year we're looking at, their revenue was quite impressive, clocking in at $3,466,600,000. They managed to secure a net profit of $160,800,000. Their assets were also substantial, totaling $15,099,000,000. Now, when it comes to the risks they face in their business journey, Beazley plc has to keep an eye on Market Risk, Credit Risk, Operational Risk, Legal/Compliance Risk, Environmental Risk, and Strategic Risk.Once upon a time, there was a company called Aurora Innovation, Inc., which focused on technology and transportation. In 2023, they made a revenue of $68 million and had a net profit or loss of $1723 million. Their assets totaled $2 billion. The business faced several risks including market risk, operational risk, legal/compliance risk, environmental risk, and strategic risk.Once upon a time, there was a company called NEW ZEALAND MEDIA AND ENTERTAINMENT, which focused mainly on media activities. In 2023, they had impressive revenue of $355,433,000 and managed to secure a net profit of $22,680,000 despite their total assets amounting to just $306,237,000. Just like any other business, they faced various risks such as Operational Risk, Legal/Compliance Risk, Market Risk, and Strategic Risk.Once upon a time, there was a company named INMUNE BIO INC., which operates in the healthcare sector. In their last fiscal year, they generated a revenue of $374,000 and had a net profit or loss of $27,299,000. Their total assets were valued at $81,795,000. Like many companies in the healthcare industry, INMUNE BIO INC. faces various business risks including Market Risk, Credit Risk, Operational Risk, Legal/Compliance Risk, and Strategic Risk.

### **Expected Example Output Format (Strictly Follow This Structure while modifying the table_names, column_names to match the given text)**:
schema = [
    {
        "table_name": "student",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "name", "type": "TEXT"},
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "course",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "title", "type": "TEXT"},
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "enrollment",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "student_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "student", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "course_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "course", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]


Now output the schema as per the system instructions.
### Output:

Output:
@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@
```json
schema = [
    {
        "table_name": "company",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "name", "type": "TEXT"},
            {"name": "industry", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "financial_year",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "year", "type": "INTEGER"},
            {"name": "revenue", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "net_profit", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "total_assets", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "risk_type", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "company_risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "company_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "company", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "risk_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "risk", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]
```

### Explanation:
1. **Company Table**: Stores information about the companies.
2. **Financial Year Table**: Tracks financial metrics for each company in a specific year.
3. **Risk Table**: Lists different types of risks faced by the companies.
4. **Company_Risk Table**: Establishes many-to-many relationships between companies and their respective risks.

This schema ensures that all entities are properly normalized, with appropriate primary keys and foreign keys to maintain referential integrity.
@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@

            {"name": "course_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "course", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]


Now output the schema as per the system instructions.
### Output:

Output:
@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@
```json
schema = [
    {
        "table_name": "company",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "name", "type": "TEXT"},
            {"name": "industry", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "financial_year",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "year", "type": "INTEGER"},
            {"name": "revenue", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "net_profit", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "total_assets", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "risk_type", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "company_risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "company_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "company", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "risk_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "risk", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]
```

### Explanation:
1. **Company Table**: Stores information about the companies.
2. **Financial Year Table**: Tracks financial metrics for each company in a specific year.
3. **Risk Table**: Lists different types of risks faced by the companies.
4. **Company_Risk Table**: Establishes many-to-many relationships between companies and their respective risks.

This schema ensures that all entities are properly normalized, with appropriate primary keys and foreign keys to maintain referential integrity.

```json
            {"name": "risk_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "risk", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]
```

### Explanation:
1. **Company Table**: Stores information about the companies.
2. **Financial Year Table**: Tracks financial metrics for each company in a specific year.
3. **Risk Table**: Lists different types of risks faced by the companies.
4. **Company_Risk Table**: Establishes many-to-many relationships between companies and their respective risks.

This schema ensures that all entities are properly normalized, with appropriate primary keys and foreign keys to maintain referential integrity.

In 2023, they had impressive revenue of $355,433,000 and managed to secure a net profit of $22,680,000 despite their total assets amounting to just $306,237,000. Just like any other business, they faced various risks such as Operational Risk, Legal/Compliance Risk, Market Risk, and Strategic Risk.Once upon a time, there was a company named INMUNE BIO INC., which operates in the healthcare sector. In their last fiscal year, they generated a revenue of $374,000 and had a net profit or loss of $27,299,000. Their total assets were valued at $81,795,000. ### **Expected Example Output Format (Strictly Follow This Structure while modifying the table_names, column_names to match the given text)**:
schema = [
    {
        "table_name": "student",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "name", "type": "TEXT"},
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "course",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "title", "type": "TEXT"},
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "enrollment",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "student_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "student", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "course_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "course", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]


Now output the schema as per the system instructions.
### Output:

Output:
@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@
```json
schema = [
    {
        "table_name": "company",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "name", "type": "TEXT"},
            {"name": "industry", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "financial_year",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "year", "type": "INTEGER"},
            {"name": "revenue", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "net_profit", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "total_assets", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "risk_type", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "company_risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "company_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "company", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "risk_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "risk", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]
```

### Explanation:
1. **Company Table**: Stores information about the companies.
2. **Financial Year Table**: Tracks financial metrics for each company in a specific year.
3. **Risk Table**: Lists different types of risks faced by the companies.
4. **Company_Risk Table**: Establishes many-to-many relationships between companies and their respective risks.

This schema ensures that all entities are properly normalized, with appropriate primary keys and foreign keys to maintain referential integrity.

            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},

```json
schema = [
    {
        "table_name": "company",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "name", "type": "TEXT"},
            {"name": "industry", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "financial_year",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "year", "type": "INTEGER"},
            {"name": "revenue", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "net_profit", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "total_assets", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "risk_type", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "company_risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "company_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "company", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "risk_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "risk", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]
```

### Explanation:
1. **Company Table**: Stores information about the companies.
2. **Financial Year Table**: Tracks financial metrics for each company in a specific year.
3. **Risk Table**: Lists different types of risks faced by the companies.
4. **Company_Risk Table**: Establishes many-to-many relationships between companies and their respective risks.

This schema ensures that all entities are properly normalized, with appropriate primary keys and foreign keys to maintain referential integrity.

```json
            {"name": "net_profit", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "total_assets", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "risk_type", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "company_risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "company_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "company", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "risk_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "risk", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]
```

### Explanation:
1. **Company Table**: Stores information about the companies.
2. **Financial Year Table**: Tracks financial metrics for each company in a specific year.
3. **Risk Table**: Lists different types of risks faced by the companies.
4. **Company_Risk Table**: Establishes many-to-many relationships between companies and their respective risks.

This schema ensures that all entities are properly normalized, with appropriate primary keys and foreign keys to maintain referential integrity.

            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "student_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "student", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "course_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "course", "foreign_key_column": "id"}

### Output:

Output:
@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@
```json
schema = [
    {
        "table_name": "company",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "name", "type": "TEXT"},
            {"name": "industry", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "financial_year",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "year", "type": "INTEGER"},
            {"name": "revenue", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "net_profit", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "total_assets", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "risk_type", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "company_risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "company_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "company", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "risk_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "risk", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]
```

### Explanation:
1. **Company Table**: Stores information about the companies.
2. **Financial Year Table**: Tracks financial metrics for each company in a specific year.
3. **Risk Table**: Lists different types of risks faced by the companies.
4. **Company_Risk Table**: Establishes many-to-many relationships between companies and their respective risks.

This schema ensures that all entities are properly normalized, with appropriate primary keys and foreign keys to maintain referential integrity.

In 2023, they had impressive revenue of $355,433,000 and managed to secure a net profit of $22,680,000 despite their total assets amounting to just $306,237,000. Just like any other business, they faced various risks such as Operational Risk, Legal/Compliance Risk, Market Risk, and Strategic Risk.Once upon a time, there was a company named INMUNE BIO INC., which operates in the healthcare sector. In their last fiscal year, they generated a revenue of $374,000 and had a net profit or loss of $27,299,000. Their total assets were valued at $81,795,000. Like many companies in the healthcare industry, INMUNE BIO INC. faces various business risks including Market Risk, Credit Risk, Operational Risk, Legal/Compliance Risk, and Strategic Risk.

schema = [
    {
        "table_name": "student",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "name", "type": "TEXT"},
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "course",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "title", "type": "TEXT"},
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "enrollment",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "student_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "student", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "course_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "course", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]


Now output the schema as per the system instructions.
### Output:

Output:
@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@
```json
schema = [
    {
        "table_name": "company",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "name", "type": "TEXT"},
            {"name": "industry", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "financial_year",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "year", "type": "INTEGER"},
            {"name": "revenue", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "net_profit", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "total_assets", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "risk_type", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "company_risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "company_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "company", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "risk_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "risk", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]
```

### Explanation:
1. **Company Table**: Stores information about the companies.
2. **Financial Year Table**: Tracks financial metrics for each company in a specific year.
3. **Risk Table**: Lists different types of risks faced by the companies.
4. **Company_Risk Table**: Establishes many-to-many relationships between companies and their respective risks.

This schema ensures that all entities are properly normalized, with appropriate primary keys and foreign keys to maintain referential integrity.

Output:
@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@
```json
schema = [
    {
        "table_name": "company",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "name", "type": "TEXT"},
            {"name": "industry", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "financial_year",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "year", "type": "INTEGER"},
            {"name": "revenue", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "net_profit", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "total_assets", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "risk_type", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "company_risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "company_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "company", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "risk_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "risk", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]
```

### Explanation:
1. **Company Table**: Stores information about the companies.
2. **Financial Year Table**: Tracks financial metrics for each company in a specific year.
3. **Risk Table**: Lists different types of risks faced by the companies.
4. **Company_Risk Table**: Establishes many-to-many relationships between companies and their respective risks.

This schema ensures that all entities are properly normalized, with appropriate primary keys and foreign keys to maintain referential integrity.

```json
schema = [
    {
        "table_name": "company",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "name", "type": "TEXT"},
            {"name": "industry", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "financial_year",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "year", "type": "INTEGER"},
            {"name": "revenue", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "net_profit", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "total_assets", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "risk_type", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "company_risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "company_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "company", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "risk_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "risk", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]
```

### Explanation:
1. **Company Table**: Stores information about the companies.
2. **Financial Year Table**: Tracks financial metrics for each company in a specific year.
3. **Risk Table**: Lists different types of risks faced by the companies.
4. **Company_Risk Table**: Establishes many-to-many relationships between companies and their respective risks.

This schema ensures that all entities are properly normalized, with appropriate primary keys and foreign keys to maintain referential integrity.

schema = [
    {
        "table_name": "student",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "name", "type": "TEXT"},
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "course",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "title", "type": "TEXT"},
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "enrollment",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "student_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "student", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "course_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "course", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]


Now output the schema as per the system instructions.
### Output:

Output:
@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@
```json
schema = [
    {
        "table_name": "company",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "name", "type": "TEXT"},
            {"name": "industry", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "financial_year",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "year", "type": "INTEGER"},
            {"name": "revenue", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "net_profit", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "total_assets", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "risk_type", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "company_risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "company_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "company", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "risk_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "risk", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]
```

### Explanation:
1. **Company Table**: Stores information about the companies.
2. **Financial Year Table**: Tracks financial metrics for each company in a specific year.
3. **Risk Table**: Lists different types of risks faced by the companies.
4. **Company_Risk Table**: Establishes many-to-many relationships between companies and their respective risks.

This schema ensures that all entities are properly normalized, with appropriate primary keys and foreign keys to maintain referential integrity.

        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "enrollment",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "student_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "student", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "course_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "course", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]


Now output the schema as per the system instructions.
### Output:

Output:
@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@
```json
schema = [
    {
        "table_name": "company",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "name", "type": "TEXT"},
            {"name": "industry", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "financial_year",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "year", "type": "INTEGER"},
            {"name": "revenue", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "net_profit", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "total_assets", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "risk_type", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "company_risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "company_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "company", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "risk_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "risk", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]
```

### Explanation:
1. **Company Table**: Stores information about the companies.
2. **Financial Year Table**: Tracks financial metrics for each company in a specific year.
3. **Risk Table**: Lists different types of risks faced by the companies.
4. **Company_Risk Table**: Establishes many-to-many relationships between companies and their respective risks.

This schema ensures that all entities are properly normalized, with appropriate primary keys and foreign keys to maintain referential integrity.

            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
```json
            {"name": "company_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "company", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "risk_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "risk", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]
```

### Explanation:
1. **Company Table**: Stores information about the companies.
2. **Financial Year Table**: Tracks financial metrics for each company in a specific year.
3. **Risk Table**: Lists different types of risks faced by the companies.
4. **Company_Risk Table**: Establishes many-to-many relationships between companies and their respective risks.

This schema ensures that all entities are properly normalized, with appropriate primary keys and foreign keys to maintain referential integrity.

However, like any large organization, it faces various business risks including Market Risk, Credit Risk, Operational Risk, Legal/Compliance Risk, Environmental Risk, and Strategic Risk.Once upon a time, there was a company called Rectifier Technologies Ltd., which focused on manufacturing and technology. In the year 2018, they made a revenue of $14,761,523.00. That same year, their net profit stood at $491,955. Their business was worth around $22,929,553.00 in total assets. Just like any other company, they faced several risks including market risk, credit risk, and operational risk.Once upon a time, there was a company named Beazley plc, which is all about finance. In the year we're looking at, their revenue was quite impressive, clocking in at $3,466,600,000. They managed to secure a net profit of $160,800,000. Their assets were also substantial, totaling $15,099,000,000. Now, when it comes to the risks they face in their business journey, Beazley plc has to keep an eye on Market Risk, Credit Risk, Operational Risk, Legal/Compliance Risk, Environmental Risk, and Strategic Risk.Once upon a time, there was a company called Aurora Innovation, Inc., which focused on technology and transportation. In 2023, they made a revenue of $68 million and had a net profit or loss of $1723 million. Their assets totaled $2 billion. The business faced several risks including market risk, operational risk, legal/compliance risk, environmental risk, and strategic risk.Once upon a time, there was a company called NEW ZEALAND MEDIA AND ENTERTAINMENT, which focused mainly on media activities. In 2023, they had impressive revenue of $355,433,000 and managed to secure a net profit of $22,680,000 despite their total assets amounting to just $306,237,000. Just like any other business, they faced various risks such as Operational Risk, Legal/Compliance Risk, Market Risk, and Strategic Risk.Once upon a time, there was a company named INMUNE BIO INC., which operates in the healthcare sector. In their last fiscal year, they generated a revenue of $374,000 and had a net profit or loss of $27,299,000. Their total assets were valued at $81,795,000. Like many companies in the healthcare industry, INMUNE BIO INC. faces various business risks including Market Risk, Credit Risk, Operational Risk, Legal/Compliance Risk, and Strategic Risk.

### **Expected Example Output Format (Strictly Follow This Structure while modifying the table_names, column_names to match the given text)**:
schema = [
    {
        "table_name": "student",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "name", "type": "TEXT"},
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "course",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "title", "type": "TEXT"},
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "enrollment",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "student_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "student", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "course_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "course", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]


Now output the schema as per the system instructions.
### Output:

Output:
@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@
```json
schema = [
    {
        "table_name": "company",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "name", "type": "TEXT"},
            {"name": "industry", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "financial_year",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "year", "type": "INTEGER"},
            {"name": "revenue", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "net_profit", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "total_assets", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "risk_type", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "company_risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "company_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "company", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "risk_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "risk", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]
```

### Explanation:
1. **Company Table**: Stores information about the companies.
2. **Financial Year Table**: Tracks financial metrics for each company in a specific year.
3. **Risk Table**: Lists different types of risks faced by the companies.
4. **Company_Risk Table**: Establishes many-to-many relationships between companies and their respective risks.

This schema ensures that all entities are properly normalized, with appropriate primary keys and foreign keys to maintain referential integrity.

```json
schema = [
    {
        "table_name": "company",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "name", "type": "TEXT"},
            {"name": "industry", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "financial_year",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "year", "type": "INTEGER"},
            {"name": "revenue", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "net_profit", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "total_assets", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "risk_type", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "company_risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "company_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "company", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "risk_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "risk", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]
```

### Explanation:
1. **Company Table**: Stores information about the companies.
2. **Financial Year Table**: Tracks financial metrics for each company in a specific year.
3. **Risk Table**: Lists different types of risks faced by the companies.
4. **Company_Risk Table**: Establishes many-to-many relationships between companies and their respective risks.

This schema ensures that all entities are properly normalized, with appropriate primary keys and foreign keys to maintain referential integrity.

```json
        "table_name": "financial_year",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "year", "type": "INTEGER"},
            {"name": "revenue", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "net_profit", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "total_assets", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "risk_type", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "company_risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "company_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "company", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "risk_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "risk", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]
```

### Explanation:
1. **Company Table**: Stores information about the companies.
2. **Financial Year Table**: Tracks financial metrics for each company in a specific year.
3. **Risk Table**: Lists different types of risks faced by the companies.
4. **Company_Risk Table**: Establishes many-to-many relationships between companies and their respective risks.

This schema ensures that all entities are properly normalized, with appropriate primary keys and foreign keys to maintain referential integrity.

### **Task:**
1. **Extract Entities & Relationships**: Identify unique entity types and relationships.
2. **Determine Attributes**: Define necessary columns for each table.
3. **Normalize the Schema**: Ensure proper **primary keys, foreign keys, and normalization (3NF)**.
4. **Generate Output in JSON Format**.
5. **Column and Table name restriction**:
reserved_sql_keywords = ["order", "group", "select", "from", "where", "join", "on", "as", "and", "or",
        "by", "insert", "update", "delete", "create", "drop", "alter", "into", "table"]
- Ensure that the table names and column names do not contain any SQL reserved keywords. 

User Prompt:

### **Text:**
Once upon a time, there was a company called Commerzbank Aktiengesellschaft that focused on finance as its principal activity. In the year they reported their financials, they had a revenue of 10,926,792,730.0 currency units and managed to achieve a net profit of 1,658,085,550 currency units. Their total assets amounted to an impressive 551,516,000,000.0 currency units. Just like any other financial institution, they faced several business risks including Market Risk, Credit Risk, Operational Risk, and Other risks.Once upon a time, there was a company called Rectifier Technologies Ltd., which focused on manufacturing and technology. In the year 2018, they made a revenue of $14,761,523.00. That same year, their net profit stood at $491,955. Their business was worth around $22,929,553.00 in total assets. Just like any other company, they faced several risks including market risk, credit risk, and operational risk.Once upon a time, there was a company named Beazley plc, which is all about finance. In the year we're looking at, their revenue was quite impressive, clocking in at $3,466,600,000. They managed to secure a net profit of $160,800,000. Their assets were also substantial, totaling $15,099,000,000. Now, when it comes to the risks they face in their business journey, Beazley plc has to keep an eye on Market Risk, Credit Risk, Operational Risk, Legal/Compliance Risk, Environmental Risk, and Strategic Risk.Once upon a time, there was a company called Aurora Innovation, Inc., which focused on technology and transportation. In 2023, they made a revenue of $68 million and had a net profit or loss of $1723 million. Their assets totaled $2 billion. The business faced several risks including market risk, operational risk, legal/compliance risk, environmental risk, and strategic risk.Once upon a time, there was a company called NEW ZEALAND MEDIA AND ENTERTAINMENT, which focused mainly on media activities. In 2023, they had impressive revenue of $355,433,000 and managed to secure a net profit of $22,680,000 despite their total assets amounting to just $306,237,000. Just like any other business, they faced various risks such as Operational Risk, Legal/Compliance Risk, Market Risk, and Strategic Risk.Once upon a time, there was a company named INMUNE BIO INC., which operates in the healthcare sector. In their last fiscal year, they generated a revenue of $374,000 and had a net profit or loss of $27,299,000. Their total assets were valued at $81,795,000. Like many companies in the healthcare industry, INMUNE BIO INC. faces various business risks including Market Risk, Credit Risk, Operational Risk, Legal/Compliance Risk, and Strategic Risk.

### **Expected Example Output Format (Strictly Follow This Structure while modifying the table_names, column_names to match the given text)**:
schema = [
    {
        "table_name": "student",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "name", "type": "TEXT"},
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "course",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "title", "type": "TEXT"},
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "enrollment",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "student_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "student", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "course_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "course", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]


Now output the schema as per the system instructions.
### Output:

Output:
@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@
```json
schema = [
    {
        "table_name": "company",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "name", "type": "TEXT"},
            {"name": "industry", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "financial_year",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "year", "type": "INTEGER"},
            {"name": "revenue", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "net_profit", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "total_assets", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "risk_type", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "company_risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "company_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "company", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "risk_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "risk", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]
```

### Explanation:
1. **Company Table**: Stores information about the companies.
2. **Financial Year Table**: Tracks financial metrics for each company in a specific year.
3. **Risk Table**: Lists different types of risks faced by the companies.
4. **Company_Risk Table**: Establishes many-to-many relationships between companies and their respective risks.

This schema ensures that all entities are properly normalized, with appropriate primary keys and foreign keys to maintain referential integrity.
@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@

```json
schema = [
    {
        "table_name": "company",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "name", "type": "TEXT"},
            {"name": "industry", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "financial_year",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "year", "type": "INTEGER"},
            {"name": "revenue", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "net_profit", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "total_assets", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "risk_type", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "company_risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "company_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "company", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "risk_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "risk", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]
```

### Explanation:
1. **Company Table**: Stores information about the companies.
2. **Financial Year Table**: Tracks financial metrics for each company in a specific year.
3. **Risk Table**: Lists different types of risks faced by the companies.
4. **Company_Risk Table**: Establishes many-to-many relationships between companies and their respective risks.

This schema ensures that all entities are properly normalized, with appropriate primary keys and foreign keys to maintain referential integrity.

            {"name": "student_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "student", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "course_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "course", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]


Now output the schema as per the system instructions.
### Output:

Output:
@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@
```json
schema = [
    {
        "table_name": "company",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "name", "type": "TEXT"},
            {"name": "industry", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "financial_year",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "year", "type": "INTEGER"},
            {"name": "revenue", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "net_profit", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "total_assets", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "risk_type", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "company_risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "company_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "company", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "risk_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "risk", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]
```

### Explanation:
1. **Company Table**: Stores information about the companies.
2. **Financial Year Table**: Tracks financial metrics for each company in a specific year.
3. **Risk Table**: Lists different types of risks faced by the companies.
4. **Company_Risk Table**: Establishes many-to-many relationships between companies and their respective risks.

This schema ensures that all entities are properly normalized, with appropriate primary keys and foreign keys to maintain referential integrity.

    }
]

### Explanation:
1. **Company Table**: Stores information about the companies.
2. **Financial Year Table**: Tracks financial metrics for each company in a specific year.
3. **Risk Table**: Lists different types of risks faced by the companies.
4. **Company_Risk Table**: Establishes many-to-many relationships between companies and their respective risks.

This schema ensures that all entities are properly normalized, with appropriate primary keys and foreign keys to maintain referential integrity.

schema = [
    {
        "table_name": "student",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "name", "type": "TEXT"},
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "course",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "title", "type": "TEXT"},
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "enrollment",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "student_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "student", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "course_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "course", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]


Now output the schema as per the system instructions.
### Output:

Output:
@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@
```json
schema = [
    {
        "table_name": "company",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "name", "type": "TEXT"},
            {"name": "industry", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "financial_year",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "year", "type": "INTEGER"},
            {"name": "revenue", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "net_profit", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "total_assets", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "risk_type", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "company_risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "company_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "company", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "risk_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "risk", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]
```

### Explanation:
1. **Company Table**: Stores information about the companies.
2. **Financial Year Table**: Tracks financial metrics for each company in a specific year.
3. **Risk Table**: Lists different types of risks faced by the companies.
4. **Company_Risk Table**: Establishes many-to-many relationships between companies and their respective risks.

This schema ensures that all entities are properly normalized, with appropriate primary keys and foreign keys to maintain referential integrity.

        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "title", "type": "TEXT"},
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "enrollment",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "student_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "student", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "course_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "course", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]


Now output the schema as per the system instructions.
### Output:

Output:
@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@@
```json
schema = [
    {
        "table_name": "company",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "name", "type": "TEXT"},
            {"name": "industry", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "financial_year",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "year", "type": "INTEGER"},
            {"name": "revenue", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "net_profit", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "total_assets", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "risk_type", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "company_risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "company_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "company", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "risk_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "risk", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]
```

### Explanation:
1. **Company Table**: Stores information about the companies.
2. **Financial Year Table**: Tracks financial metrics for each company in a specific year.
3. **Risk Table**: Lists different types of risks faced by the companies.
4. **Company_Risk Table**: Establishes many-to-many relationships between companies and their respective risks.

This schema ensures that all entities are properly normalized, with appropriate primary keys and foreign keys to maintain referential integrity.

```json
            {"name": "company_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "company", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "risk_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "risk", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]
```

### Explanation:
1. **Company Table**: Stores information about the companies.
2. **Financial Year Table**: Tracks financial metrics for each company in a specific year.
3. **Risk Table**: Lists different types of risks faced by the companies.
4. **Company_Risk Table**: Establishes many-to-many relationships between companies and their respective risks.

This schema ensures that all entities are properly normalized, with appropriate primary keys and foreign keys to maintain referential integrity.

```json
schema = [
    {
        "table_name": "company",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "name", "type": "TEXT"},
            {"name": "industry", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "financial_year",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "year", "type": "INTEGER"},
            {"name": "revenue", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "net_profit", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"},
            {"name": "total_assets", "type": "DECIMAL(15,2)"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "risk_type", "type": "TEXT"}
        ]
    },
    {
        "table_name": "company_risk",
        "columns": [
            {"name": "id", "type": "INTEGER", "primary_key": True},
            {"name": "company_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "company", "foreign_key_column": "id"},
            {"name": "risk_id", "type": "INTEGER", "foreign_key": True, "foreign_key_table": "risk", "foreign_key_column": "id"}
        ]
    }
]
```

### Explanation:
1. **Company Table**: Stores information about the companies.
2. **Financial Year Table**: Tracks financial metrics for each company in a specific year.
3. **Risk Table**: Lists different types of risks faced by the companies.
4. **Company_Risk Table**: Establishes many-to-many relationships between companies and their respective risks.

This schema ensures that all entities are properly normalized, with appropriate primary keys and foreign keys to maintain referential integrity.
